(34)

where the components of the spatial curvature tensor
\mathchoice{P^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}{k}{l}}}}{P^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}{k}{l}}}}{P^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}{k}{l}}}}{P^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}{k}{l}}}}
are evaluated on the reference curve (along which
\mathchoice{x^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=0
).
If motion takes place only along the reference curve, the exact equations of motion (
13
) in a static reference frame take the form

\displaystyle\ddot{z}

\displaystyle=-(1-\dot{z}^{2}/c^{2})\varg(z)\,,

(35a)

\displaystyle\dot{t}

\displaystyle=\exp[-\varphi(z)/c^{2}]\,,

(35b)

where dots indicate derivatives with respect to the temporal parameter
T
, and
\varg(z)=\mathrm{d}\varphi(z)/\mathrm{d}z
[the sign convention chosen here is such that
\varg>0
corresponds to a downward-pointing acceleration].
Although these equations generally do not admit closed-form solutions, for practical applications it suffices to use short-time expansions of the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}t(T)&=t_{0}+e^{-\varphi(z_{0})/c^{2}}\bigg[T-\frac{1}{2!}\frac{\varv_{0}\varg_{0}}{c^{2}}T^{2}\\
&\hskip 40.00006pt+\frac{1}{3!}\left(\frac{\varg_{0}^{2}}{c^{2}}-\gamma_{0}\frac{\varv_{0}^{2}}{c^{2}}\right)T^{3}+O(T^{4})\bigg]\,,\end{split}

(36a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}z(T)&=z_{0}+\varv_{0}T-\left(1-\frac{\varv_{0}^{2}}{c^{2}}\right)\bigg[\frac{\varg_{0}}{2}T^{2}\\
&\hskip 50.00008pt+\frac{\varv_{0}}{3}\left(\frac{\varg_{0}^{2}}{c^{2}}+\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2}\right)T^{3}+O(T^{4})\bigg]\,,\end{split}

(36b)

where
z_{0}
and
\varv_{0}
denote the initial position and vertical velocity, respectively, and
\varg_{0}=\varphi^{\prime}(z_{0})
and
\gamma_{0}=\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z_{0})
denote the vertical gravitational acceleration and gravity gradient, respectively.
For simplicity of exposition, the following analysis is carried out in the limit
c\to\infty
, in which case
Eq.

36
reduce to the trajectories known from Newtonian mechanics.

Whereas
Eqs.

11
and
12
suffice for computing the phase shifts in the experiments described above, this is no longer the case here since the atom-light interactions produce non-trivial phase shifts.
Specifically, the interaction of the atoms with
\pi
- and
\pi/2
-pulses produces additional phase shifts of the form

\displaystyle\Delta\Psi_{*}=\sum_{i}\left[\mathchoice{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}\,\Psi_{\textsc{em}}(\mathchoice{t^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{t^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{t^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{t^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}},\mathchoice{z^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{z^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{z^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{z^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}})-\mathchoice{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}\,\Psi_{\textsc{em}}(\mathchoice{t^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{t^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{t^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{t^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}},\mathchoice{z^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{z^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{z^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{z^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}})\right]\,.

(37)

Here,
i
extends over all atom-light interactions at the coordinates
(\mathchoice{t^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{t^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{t^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{t^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}},\mathchoice{z^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{z^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{z^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{z^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}})
in the upper arm and at
(\mathchoice{t^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{t^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{t^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{t^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}},\mathchoice{z^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{z^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{z^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{z^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}})
in the lower arm,
\Psi_{\textsc{em}}
denotes the phase of the electromagnetic field, and the integers
\mathchoice{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}
and
\mathchoice{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}
depend on the details of the interaction
Peters et al. 2001
;
Altin et al. 2013
:
For Bragg transitions, the absolute values of
\mathchoice{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}
and
\mathchoice{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}
indicate the order of the transition, whereas
|\mathchoice{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}|=|\mathchoice{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}|=1
for Raman transitions, and the signs of
\mathchoice{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{i}}}}_{{{i}}}}
and
\mathchoice{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}{n^{{{\textsc{ii}}}}_{{{i}}}}
indicate whether the atom’s momentum along the gradient of
\Psi_{\textsc{em}}
increases or decreases in the process of the interaction.
A heuristic derivation of
Eq.

37
can be found in Ref.
Hogan et al. 2009
; more detailed derivations for Bragg and Raman transitions are provided in Ref.
Bernhardt and Shore 1981
and Ref.
Moler et al. 1992
, respectively.
If Raman transitions are used, additional phase shifts arise if the two interfering rays spend unequal times in the various internal energy states. However, since the experiments considered here have equal time intervals between the light-matter interactions, these additional phase contributions vanish
Wolf et al. 2011
.

(b)

Figure 7:

Schematic space-time diagrams of atomic fountain gravimeters in the absence (a) or presence (b) of a uniform vertical acceleration
\varg
.
Atoms are launched into a vacuum tube and manipulated using a sequence of laser pulses.
Regardless of the vertical acceleration
\varg
, both rays take equal amounts of proper time.
However, the value of
\varg
determines the position of the light-matter interactions and thus also determines the phase shifts acquired in the course of these interaction events.

Gravimetry

In their 1991 paper, Kasevich and Chu reported on the first atomic-fountain-experiment capable of functioning as a gravimeter
Kasevich and Chu 1991
. While this experiment used stimulated Raman transitions (see Ref.
Kasevich and Chu 1992
for a detailed model of the experiment), in 2013, Altin
et al
. reported on a refined experiment using Bragg transitions instead
Altin et al. 2013
.
Simplified space-time diagrams of these experiments are shown in
Fig.

7
: a
\tfrac{1}{2}\pi
-pulse at
T=0
acts as a beam splitter to produce atomic wave packets that propagate upwards with different velocities
\varv_{0}
and
\varv_{0}+\Delta\varv_{\text{r}}
, respectively, where
\Delta\varv_{\text{r}}=n\hslash k_{\textsc{em}}/m
is the recoil velocity (in which
n\equiv\mathchoice{n^{{{1}}}_{{{\textsc{i}}}}}{n^{{{1}}}_{{{\textsc{i}}}}}{n^{{{1}}}_{{{\textsc{i}}}}}{n^{{{1}}}_{{{\textsc{i}}}}}
denotes the order of the diffraction process). After the “interrogation time”
T_{*}
, the wave packets are subject to a
\pi
-pulse that changes the velocities by
\pm n\hslash k_{\textsc{em}}/m
. The wave packets meet at
T=2T_{*}
, where a final
\tfrac{1}{2}\pi
-pulse brings them to equal final velocities in each output arm, after which interference is observed.
Integrating
Eq.

7b
along the trajectories given by
Eq.

36
shows that
\Delta\Psi_{\zeta}
vanishes if gravity gradients and
\varv^{2}/c^{2}
-corrections are negligible. This is in agreement with calculations based on the Schrödinger equation for a particle in a linear gravitational potential
Giulini 2012
. Assuming low velocities, i.e.,
\varv\ll c
, and an electromagnetic phase of the form
\Psi_{\textsc{em}}=\omega_{\textsc{em}}(z/c-t)
, one obtains

\displaystyle\Delta\Psi_{\zeta}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(38a)

\displaystyle\Delta\Psi_{*}

\displaystyle=-\varg m\Delta\varv_{\text{r}}T_{*}^{2}/\hslash^{2}=-n\,\boldsymbol{\varg}\cdot\boldsymbol{k}_{\textsc{em}}T_{*}^{2}\,.

(38b)

Hence, the overall phase shift is structurally identical to that of
Eq.

33
.
The interpretation of this phase shift was debated in the literature
Müller et al. 2010a
;
Wolf et al. 2010
;
Müller et al. 2010b
;
Wolf et al. 2010
;
Wolf et al. 2011
;
Sinha and Samuel 2011
;
Giulini 2012
;
Wolf et al. 2012
;
Schleich et al. 2013
.
Specifically, in view of
Eq.

7b
, Müller
et al
. interpreted the phase shift as a measure of proper time tracked by a “Compton clock”
Müller et al. 2010a
. However, Sinha and Samuel
Sinha and Samuel 2011
as well as Giulini
Giulini 2012
argued that
Eq.

7b
does not describe any observable periodic changes in internal degrees of freedom. Moreover, Wolf
et al
. showed that both interfering rays have equal proper times
Wolf et al. 2010
;
Wolf et al. 2011
. This last observation is consistent with
\Delta\Psi_{\zeta}=0
derived here.

In a subsequent experiment, Asenbaum
et al
.
Asenbaum et al. 2020
compared high-precision phase measurements from an atom cloud of
87
Rb to that of
85
Rb in order to measure differential gravitational accelerations between the two isotopes. The results showed no deviations from universality of free fall to the level of
${10}^{-12}$\varg_{\text{std}}
, where
\varg_{\text{std}}
denotes the standard acceleration of gravity, giving the highest sensitivity laboratory test of the weak equivalence principle to date. The atom clouds (
\sim${10}^{5}$
atoms per cloud) undergo evaporative cooling before being launched from the atom trap to a height of
8.6\text{\,}\mathrm{m}
. With a total free-fall time of
2T_{*}=$1910\text{\,}\mathrm{ms}$
, the experiment had a single-shot acceleration sensitivity of
$1.4\text{\times}{10}^{-11}$\varg_{\text{std}}
. Experimental challenges to reach this sensitivity involve an
ac
-Stark shift generated by the atom launch optics, which can be reduced by improved laser systems; differential magnetic gradients across the drop tower, mitigated significantly by generating a uniform magnetic field across the experiment with a solenoid; and imaging errors, which are addressed by calibration of the atom imaging optics.

(a)

(b)

Figure 8:

Schematic space-time diagram of interfering rays in an atomic fountain in the absence (a) or presence (b) of gravity gradients.
If the relative recoil velocities are in the ratio
1:-2:1
, the rays interfering at the output are separated at the input by a height difference that depends on the gravity gradient.

Gradiometry

Using multiple gravimeters at different heights, it is possible to measure gravity gradients
Snadden et al. 1998
;
Peters et al. 2001
;
McGuirk et al. 2002
;
Fixler et al. 2007
;
Sorrentino et al. 2012
;
Asenbaum et al. 2017
and even second spatial derivatives of the gravitational acceleration
Rosi et al. 2015
.
Additionally, it is possible to extend the free-fall heights of atomic-fountain experiments so that higher-order terms in
Eq.

36
become relevant.
Specifically, if
T^{3}
-terms are retained in the perturbative expansion and if the same electromagnetic pulse sequence is used as for the gravimeters described above, the rays interfering at the output must have a vertical offset
\Delta z_{0}=\gamma\Delta\varv_{\text{r}}T_{*}^{3}+O(T_{*}^{4})
at the input
Bordé 2001
, see
Fig.

8
.
In this case (assuming, as above,
\varv\ll c
), the total phase shift takes the form
\Delta\Psi=\Delta\Psi_{\zeta}+\Delta\Psi_{*}
with

\displaystyle\Delta\Psi_{\zeta}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{2}\gamma m\Delta\varv_{\text{r}}^{2}T_{*}^{3}/\hslash+O(T_{*}^{4})\,,

(39a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta\Psi_{*}&=-\varg m\Delta\varv_{\text{r}}T_{*}^{2}/\hslash\\
&\quad+\gamma m\Delta\varv_{\text{r}}(\varv_{0}+\Delta\varv_{\text{r}})T_{*}^{3}/\hslash+O(T_{*}^{4})\,,\end{split}

(39b)

in agreement with calculations based on the Schrödinger equation with a Newtonian potential
Wolf and Tourrenc 1999
.
Alternatively, the frequencies of the electromagnetic pulses can be adjusted (depending on the gravity gradient
\gamma
) to make the wave packets overlap at both the input and the output. In this case, the total phase shift takes the same value as given here
Wolf et al. 2011
.
Such a gravity-gradient-induced phase shift was measured by Overstreet
et al
. in 2022
Overstreet et al. 2022
.

Atomic fountain experiments of this kind are candidates for many future gravitational experiments. One such proposal suggests using atomic fountains to detect gravitational waves (
gw
), allowing measurement of signals out of reach for current optical detection systems
Dimopoulos et al. 2009
. These include both terrestrial and space-based detection systems, consisting of multiple atom interferometers measuring variations in the timing of the laser pulses caused by a
gw
passing through the interferometer. Other space-based atom interferometry experiments have been proposed for further tests of the equivalence principle and dark matter searches
Tino et al. 2019
. Recent advancements in atom lattice traps are allowing the possibility of gravitational measurements with trapped atoms
Panda et al. 2024
. These experiments have the advantage of extended interaction times of tens of seconds. However, a theoretical description of these guided atom evolution is beyond the scope of this paper, and is one avenue for future expansion of the framework described here.

VII
Discussion

In this paper, we developed a unified theoretical framework for describing quantum interference in both massless and massive systems in the presence of external stationary gravitational fields. Compared to perturbative weak-field expansions, our geometric approach offers a simpler and more transparent description, reducing interpretational ambiguities:

•

Our geometric description does not rely on any flat reference geometry and thus eliminates complications in modeling experimental setups using charts for which coordinate differences and lengths computed using a flat reference metric
\mathchoice{\eta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\eta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\eta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\eta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}
differ from physical lengths computed using the full metric
\mathchoice{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}
, c.f., e.g., Ref.
Beig et al. 2018
.

•

This approach makes it evident that details of the spatial metric tensor do not matter for the description of the experiments described here. In particular, none of the experiments discussed here are sensitive to the parameter
\gamma_{\textsc{ppn}}
arising in the parameterized post-Newtonian (
ppn
) framework. Previous derivations of this statement were restricted to optical interferometry and required significantly more extensive computations
Brodutch et al. 2015
;
Mieling 2022
.

•

Similarly, the general equations describing the phase evolution of massive and massless particles do not involve the space-time curvature tensor. Hence, curvature components can only be inferred by indirect means, e.g., by varying external parameters such as the spatial extent of the interferometer. In this context, we derived an exact formula that relates specific components of the space-time curvature tensor to the gravitational acceleration and its gradient, see
Eq.

5
. This provides a rigorous basis for the commonly used heuristic of interpreting gravity gradients as an indicator for curvature.

•

Moreover, our computation of lapse-induced phases in photons does not rely on heuristic methods such as effective refractive indices arising from a gravitational potential, nor do they require assigning to photons an effective gravitational mass.

We derived general formulas for the phase evolution of different quantum systems and used them to analyze various experimental configurations.
For experiments with photons, we showed that free-space and fiber-based interferometers yield essentially the same detectable lapse-induced phase shift. In both scenarios, the use of entangled quantum states provides new insights into the gravitational coupling as the resulting detection probabilities cannot be mimicked by classical field intensities. In experiments where gradients of the lapse-induced phase shifts are measurable, such setups enable the direct observation of specific components of the Riemann curvature tensor with non-classical probes. Our geometric approach also yields predictions for phase shifts in experiments that probe gravity using unguided matter waves instead of photons. The phase shifts we derive for Mach–Zehnder, Bonse–Hart, and atomic fountain configurations not only agree with previous results obtained assuming either Newtonian gravity or linearized Einsteinian gravity, but our method also extends these results to arbitrary stationary gravitational fields. The interpretation of the phase shifts arising for both the optical and matter-wave experiments follows naturally from our description. While gravitational phase shifts in optical interferometers arise directly from the lapse function, the situation is more subtle for matter-wave interferometers. In matter-wave gravimeters, the lapse-induced phase shift vanishes and the observed phase originates from the interaction with light pulses at different locations that depend on the gravitational acceleration. However, when gravity gradients are taken into account, lapse-induced phase shifts re-emerge in addition to the phase from the light-matter interaction and are directly proportional to the gravity gradient. Our methods can be extended to include internal degrees of freedom and also guided motion of massive particles.

where
\mathchoice{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}=\mathchoice{\eta^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\eta^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\eta^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\eta^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{K^{{{0}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{K^{{{0}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{K^{{{0}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{K^{{{0}}}_{{{\nu}}}}
is the “unperturbed” wave-vector and
\mathchoice{\kappa^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\kappa^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\kappa^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\kappa^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}
is any one-form satisfying
\mathchoice{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}\mathchoice{\kappa^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\kappa^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\kappa^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\kappa^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}=0
.
For any two points
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
lying along an integral curve
\Gamma
of
\mathchoice{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}
,
Eq.

6
yields

\displaystyle\Psi(p_{2})=\Psi(p_{1})+\int_{\Gamma}[\mathchoice{K^{{{0}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{K^{{{0}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{K^{{{0}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{K^{{{0}}}_{{{\mu}}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathchoice{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}\mathchoice{\bar{K}^{{{\nu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\nu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\nu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\nu}}}_{{{0}}}}]\mathchoice{\mathrm{d}x^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\ma (41)

in which
\mathchoice{\kappa^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\kappa^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\kappa^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\kappa^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}
does not contribute because of its orthogonality to
\mathchoice{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{0}}}}

(

, Sect. 8)
.
When using this equation, care must be taken to use appropriate curves along which the integrals are to be evaluated.
In particular, these curves
cannot
be prescribed independently of
\mathchoice{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}
.
This can be seen, for example, by studying the transformation of the integral arising in
Eq.

41
under linearized diffeomorphisms, under which
\mathchoice{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}
changes as
\text{\textdelta}\mathchoice{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}=\mathchoice{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\partial^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\partial^{{\mathc _{{{\nu}}}}\mathchoice{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}
. If the curve
\Gamma
were invariant under such gauge transformations, then the integral
I_{\Gamma}=\int_{\Gamma}[\mathchoice{K^{{{0}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{K^{{{0}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{K^{{{0}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{K^{{{0}}}_{{{\mu}}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathchoice{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}\mathchoice{\bar{K}^{{{\nu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\nu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\nu}}}_{{{0}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\nu}}}_{{{0}}}}]\mathchoice{\mathrm{d}x^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scri would transforms as

\displaystyle\text{\textdelta}I_{\Gamma}=(\mathchoice{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}})(p_{2})-(\mathchoice{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{K}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\ma (42)

where
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
are the initial and final endpoints of
\Gamma
, respectively.
This shows that gauge-invariant predictions for phase shifts can be computed using
Eq.

41
only if the coordinate expressions for the curves involved are gauge-
dependent
.

To illustrate this point, consider
Eq.

41
applied to null rays with (angular) coordinate frequency
\omega
propagating along straight coordinate lines joining the points

\displaystyle(\mathchoice{x^{{{i}}}_{{{A}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{{A}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{{A}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{{A}}}})

\displaystyle=(0,0,0)\,,

\displaystyle(\mathchoice{x^{{{i}}}_{{{B}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{{B}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{{B}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{{B}}}})

\displaystyle=(0,0,h)\,,

(43a)

\displaystyle(\mathchoice{x^{{{i}}}_{{{C}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{{C}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{{C}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{{C}}}})

\displaystyle=(l,0,0)\,,

\displaystyle(\mathchoice{x^{{{i}}}_{{{D}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{{D}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{{D}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{{D}}}})

\displaystyle=(l,0,h)\,.

(43b)

If
\mathchoice{l^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{l^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{l^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{l^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}=\mathchoice{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}
, the parameters
h
and
l
accurately represent the physical arm lengths of the interferometer.
Integrating (
41
) in the linear potential
\varphi=\varg z
, one obtains the same result as in
Eq.

27
.
If, instead, the metric is given by
\mathchoice{l^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{l^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{l^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{l^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}=(1+2\varg z/c^{2})\mathchoice{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}
, which corresponds to a post-Newtonian metric with a linear potential
(
Misner et al. 1973
, §18.4)
, then the geodesic distances of the “horizontal” segments are unequal:

\displaystyle l_{AC}

\displaystyle\approx l\,,

\displaystyle l_{BD}

\displaystyle\approx(1+\varg h/c^{2})l\,.

(44)

This mismatch causes an additional phase shift
\Delta\Psi_{\text{mismatch}}=-\omega(l_{AC}-l_{BD})/c\approx\omega\varg hl/c^{3}
, so that the overall phase shift is twice as large as that in
Eq.

27
:

\displaystyle\Delta\Psi_{\text{imbalanced}}

\displaystyle\approx-2\varg hl\omega/c^{3}\,.

(45)

Identical coordinate expressions for the spatial curves in
Eq.

41
thus yield different phase shifts depending on the explicit form of
\mathchoice{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\varepsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}
, despite the fact that the two fields are related by a gauge transformation, namely that generated by

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}}}}

\displaystyle=\varg xz\,,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}}}}

\displaystyle=\varg yz\,,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\varg(z^{2}-x^{2}-y^{2})\,.

(46)

This analysis also shows that experimental tests of
Eq.

27
require precise control over the interferometer’s arm lengths, as relative imbalances of
\varg h/c^{2}\approx${10}^{-16}$(h/$\mathrm{m}$)
may double or cancel the phase shift induced by the gravitational redshift.
